    - `pin_sum` decrements use the quantised `p_in` (±1/30 per masked posting).
    - With `--extra-index`, the mask drops hashes for every index but adjusts only the first index's units.
    - As with joint queries, `gather_rank` and `present_prob` of unmasked units change, because masked units leave the sample-wide gather order and presence prior; every other column is unchanged (tested).
  - Tests: windows cover every stretch of overlap + 1 nt and never join records; on an index of the 20 fixture proteins and a "genome" of CDSs 0–9 (5–9 on the reverse strand) plus half of CDS 10, units 0–9 are masked whole and `host_like`, unit 10 partly, and the adjusted `m_g` and `pin_hist` equal the postings left after dropping the masked hashes (tier 2, and with a dense tier); the masked query has no hits on units 0–9 and leaves units 11–19 unchanged; CLI `mask` and `query --mask`.
  - *Not done:* the released human mask (T2T-CHM13 + rCRS chrM + PhiX174) is built on HPC by step 6's workflow; the mask's genome and checksum in the query output wait for the output metadata of the spec.

* **Phase 7, step 5 — Pfam truth and Pfam scoring in the fmh benchmark (the phase-5 item phase 7's decisions need).**
  - *Annotation:* `PFAM_PROTEINS` deals every gene's protein in the KEGG extraction (from the genomes' mapping tables) into `--pfam_chunks` FASTA files. `PFAM_ANNOTATE` runs `hmmsearch --cut_ga` on each (HMMER 3.4 container), and `PFAM_DOMAINS` writes `domains.parquet` (gene, Pfam accession without version, envelope in aa) and `pfam_members.parquet`. `--pfam_url` defaults to the current Pfam release; it should be set to the release behind MGnify's `mgy_proteins_pfam` (not recorded in the release notes we have; to check).
  - *Truth:* `genes.parquet` gains the strand. Each domain's nt span on the genome is `start + 3·aa` on the forward strand and `end − 3·aa` reversed on the reverse strand. Pfam truth is `truth` over those spans: present if a primary alignment overlaps a domain, depth = Σ aligned bases / domain length. Truth files now name their key column `label` (old files with `ko_id` still read).
  - *Profiles:*
    - *Pfam units* (`pfam_<name>`, one per `--indexes` entry): each domain is a member of its Pfam's unit.
    - *MGnify90 indexes* (`--mgnify_indexes`): `pfam-profile` sums the unit profile per Pfam through `unit_pfam.parquet`, each unit counting for each of its Pfams. MGnify stores accessions as integers, which become `PF%05d`.
    - `score` and `summary` gain `label` and `arm` columns. Other tools stay on KO for now (DIAMOND needs Pfam on the KEGG reference proteins, HUMAnN its UniRef90 → Pfam regroup).
  - *Fixture:* `tests/data/mini_fmh/Pfam-mini.hmm`, six single-sequence HMMs (`hmmbuild`, with GA thresholds) from segments of mini-fixture proteins, two from one protein (`make_fixtures.mini_pfam`).
  - *Tests:* every domain's genome span translates back to the domain on either strand; reads over a domain make its Pfam present and reads elsewhere do not; chunked annotation gives the same domains; per-Pfam sums with multi-label units and integer accessions.
  - *Test profile run* (`-profile test,docker`, local): both labels scored end to end; Pfam units on the mini fixture at purity and completeness 1.0.
  - *Fixed on the way:*
    - `query --draws` crashed when nothing was detected (an index the reads miss, or host-only reads), in the posterior's empty batch; the posterior is now skipped then.
    - Empty profile TSVs read as strings in `bench.py` (`read_profile`).
    - `--tools ''` arrived as `true` and was rejected; it now means no tools.

* **Phase 7, step 6 — the ablations as HPC runs of the fmh benchmark (`run_ablations.sh index|reads|host OUTDIR`; README, Ablations). Set up, not run; revised in step 19 before launch.**
  - *Query arms* (`--query_arms`, config only): `[name, args, reads, mask, decoy]`. Each arm is a set of `query` options on raw, fastp (`--trim_poly_g`) or hostile-cleaned reads (bowtie2, `human-t2t-hla`), optionally with the host mask sidecar (`MASK`, per index built in the run) and the human-proteome decoy (`DECOY_INDEX`, UniProt UP000005640, one unit per protein, built with the index config's own options) queried jointly. PROFILE, SCORE and the published names carry the arm (`seedN_<index>~<arm>`); `DETECTED` runs for the plain arm only.
  - *Host spike-in* (`--host_fractions`):
    - `HOST_GENOME`: T2T-CHM13v2.0 plus rCRS chrM and PhiX174. iss abundances follow record length, with the mitochondrion at 100 copies and PhiX at 0.5% of host reads.
    - `HOST_READS`: one set of host reads, with the metagenomes' read model.
    - `MIX`: one sample per seed and share, `seed<N>h<percent>`. *Amendment:* depth is held constant: microbial pairs are kept with probability 1 − *f* and host pairs added to the same total, rather than adding host reads on top. A 99% sample is then still ~1 Gbp, not ~100 Gbp, and the truth is recomputed on the mixed reads.
  - *Score metrics added:* `split_l1` (per component with ≥ 2 detected units, the L1 between estimated and true shares, weighted by the component's true depth), `group_size_mean` and `host_like_detected`.
  - *Three runs:*
    - `index`: 15 configs × 2 labels + `fmh_compat`. Base k 11 / 20 letters / *t\_base* 1/1000 / *n\_min* 8, and one change each: floors, *t\_base*, dense tier, k, Murphy-10, Dayhoff.
    - `reads`: frames × QC arms on two indexes, once per read model (novaseq, miseq).
    - `host`: 4 shares × 5 host-handling arms.
    - Estimator ablations are the `abundance` rows of every run (`--all-estimators`).
  - *Checked:*
    - Stub runs of all three give the expected sizes (310, 550, 480 profiles).
    - A real local run of every arm except hostile (`-profile test,docker` with a 60 kb random "host" and the fixture proteins as decoy) completes. On that toy fixture all arms keep purity and completeness 1.0. It checks the plumbing, not the method.
  - *Fixed on the way:* the test profile's fixed `storeDir` (`results-test/db`) keeps files from `-stub-run` runs, which later real runs then reuse as empty databases. Stub checks should pass their own `--db_dir`.
  - *Not covered here:* the divergence ladder (held-out genomes with relatives at known identity) and containment-AAI calibration, which go to the simulation benchmark (step 7); other tools on Pfam.

* **Phase 7, step 7 — frame modes and containment AAI on the simulation benchmark (run locally; the first ablation results).**
  - *Code (`sim.py`):*
    - `--frames` queries every config in each frame mode.
    - `complete_<bin>` and `bias_len_<bin>` score completeness, and the median estimate / depth of true positives over the overall median, by protein length (200–300, 300–450, 450–600 aa).
    - On `gather_zi` rows, AAI is scored against exact truth (substitutions only, so identity is per position): bias against the strain's mean identity to its unit's members (per strain-to-centroid identity), against the nearest member and against the centroid; Spearman; interval coverage of both targets.
  - *Run:* defaults (100 families × 3 paralogs × 4 members at 97%, half of the units present at 85/90/95/100% identity, 0.2% substitution errors, 20% random reads), 3 seeds, `--draws 30`. Configs `dense`, `s10`, `floor`, `floor_d20`, `floor_d100`; frames `stopfree`, `edges:15/20/30`, `all`. Outputs in `results/sim-phase7*` (not committed).
  - **Frame mode** (`gather_zi`, means over 3 seeds):

    | Config | Frames | Purity | Completeness | Completeness 200–300 aa | L1 | log-ratio sd |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | floor | stopfree | 0.943 | 0.842 | 0.858 | 0.392 | 0.763 |
    | floor | edges:20 | 0.942 | 0.871 | 0.906 | 0.355 | 0.770 |
    | floor | all | 0.942 | 0.871 | 0.906 | 0.354 | 0.772 |
    | floor\_d20 | stopfree | 0.943 | 0.842 | 0.858 | 0.281 | 0.539 |
    | floor\_d20 | edges:20 | 0.942 | 0.871 | 0.906 | 0.244 | 0.503 |
    | s10 | stopfree | 0.969 | 0.967 | 0.933 | 0.243 | 0.840 |
    | s10 | edges:20 | 0.969 | 0.978 | 0.953 | 0.214 | 0.749 |
    | dense | stopfree | 0.898 | 0.996 | 0.993 | 0.310 | 0.576 |
    | dense | edges:20 | 0.879 | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.308 | 0.485 |
    | dense | all | 0.869 | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.335 | 0.479 |

    - *Edges help:* +3 points of completeness on the floored index (+5 on the shortest proteins), L1 −10% (floor) to −13% (floor\_d20), at no purity cost below the dense index.
    - *Edges match all six frames in accuracy on every sparse index, and beat them on the dense one:* `all` loses 3 points of purity there and raises L1. `edges:15` ≈ `edges:20`; `edges:30` is slightly worse.
    - *The predicted length bias does not show in abundance:* `bias_len_*` stays within ±4% for every mode. The zero-inflated fit counts unhit end k-mers as absent (lower `present`), not as low coverage, so stop-free mainly costs completeness and precision, not bias.
    - *Provisional:* `edges:20` as the default, pending the fmh benchmark (`reads` run: error model, Pfam) and its hashing cost.
  - **Containment AAI** (`gather_zi` true positives with an `aai`):

    | Config | Median bias vs centroid | vs mean member | vs nearest member | Spearman (mean member) | Interval coverage (centroid) |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | dense | −0.003 | +0.025 | +0.023 | 0.855 | 0.54 |
    | floor\_d20 | −0.003 | +0.024 | +0.024 | 0.845 | 0.56 |
    | floor\_d100 | — | +0.025 | +0.023 | 0.876 | — |
    | floor (no dense tier) | +0.013 | +0.03 (100%) to +0.16 (85%) | +0.041 | 0.44 | — |

    - *What `aai` estimates:* identity to the cluster's consensus (here its centroid), unbiased to 0.003 with a dense tier at every identity from 85 to 100%. It is not the identity to an average or nearest member: a strain identical to the centroid shares almost all its k-mers with the union of four 97% members, so `copies` ≈ 1. This is bias 2 of Sequence similarity (union mosaic), and it is a constant offset here (+0.025 = 1 − the members' identity). *Amendment to the column's meaning:* read `aai` as AAI to the cluster's consensus. For calibration on real data, the truth to compare against is identity to the representative, not to members.
    - *Without a dense tier `aai` is not usable* (bias up to +0.16 at 85%, Spearman 0.44), as Sequence similarity predicted for ~8 kept k-mers.
    - *Intervals are too narrow:* 0.54–0.56 coverage against an unbiased target. They carry the read-sampling uncertainty of the present k-mers, not the randomness of which k-mers survive a given identity (binomial at least, more with overlapping windows). The fix is a draw of the containment around *a*^k in each posterior draw; it waits for the calibration on the fmh divergence ladder, together with the gate (|bias| ≤ 0.01: met against the consensus; coverage ≥ 0.9: not met; Spearman ≥ 0.9: 0.85, over a ladder of only four identities and member noise).
    - `aai_naive`: +0.03 against the mean member, the same offset.

* **Phase 7, step 8 — closed-form `aai_lo`/`aai_hi` (no draws).** `--draws` stays 0 by default, so AAI intervals no longer come from the posterior. `aai_interval` computes them from the zero-inflated fit. This supersedes step 2's posterior interval, which covered 0.55 in step 7. Copies *c* estimates *a*^k, and its variance adds three terms:
  - *survival*: *c*(1 − *c*) *d* / `pin_sum`, from where a strain's substitutions fall. *d* = 1 + 2*t* Σ\_{j=1}^{k−1} (*a*^j − *a*^k)/(1 − *a*^k) for the correlation of windows *j* apart, with *t* = *m* / `n_kmers` the chance a neighbouring window is kept too. *d* → k for dense windows near *a* = 1 and → 1 for sparse ones; it is derived exactly, not fitted. A Monte Carlo test with random substitutions puts 92–98% of strains inside.
  - *read sampling of the present k-mers*: Binomial(present × *m*, seen).
  - *the coverage estimate*: the zero-truncated Poisson Fisher information, by the delta method.
  - Both sampling terms are scaled by a clumping factor 1 + μ. A read hits a run of neighbouring kept k-mers, μ per covering read, recovered from tier-2 hits per hitting read (a zero-truncated mean) and scaled to the fitted tier. The interval is *a* ± z·sd on the AAI scale (delta method), clipped to [0, 1]. Null where `aai` is null.
  - *How it got there* (`results/sim-aai-ci`, dense config, coverage against centroid identity at 85/90/95/100%):
    - posterior draws (step 2): 0.55 overall;
    - survival + imputation terms: 0.94/0.93/0.87/0.27. Strains at 100% identity and depth ~1 had `present_zi` 0.1–0.3: the k-mers hit come in runs from few reads, so the sample size is the reads;
    - + read clumping: 100% strains still outside, because a Beta's quantiles never reach 1;
    - normal on the containment scale: 0.93–0.99, but lower bounds of 0 at 85% (*c* ≈ 0.17);
    - normal on the AAI scale: 0.96–1.0, too wide on dense tiers, because the hit-sampling term counted absent k-mers again; fixed to Binomial(present × *m*, seen);
    - clumping as 1 + μ instead of hits per hitting read, which overstated it on sparse tier 2.
  - **Result** (3 seeds, `gather_zi` true positives with an `aai`):
    - coverage of the centroid identity: dense 0.97/0.99/0.93/0.87 at 85/90/95/100%, median width 0.12/0.09/0.07/0.03;
    - `floor_d20`: 1.0/1.0/0.94/0.92, width 0.13/0.11/0.09/0.05;
    - by coverage bin, 0.92–1.0 throughout, widest below 1× (0.17–0.19).
    - Slightly conservative at high coverage, because the simulation places an exact number of substitutions, which varies less than the random-substitution model. Real substitutions cluster in variable regions, which pushes the other way. The floored index without a dense tier reports intervals too, wide as expected.
  - Tests: the interval holds its point and stays within [0, 1] on the fixture profile; the survival variance matches simulated substitutions (92–98% inside); more kept windows give narrower intervals, and sparse windows narrower than dense ones at equal counts.

* **Phase 7, step 9 — the simulation ladder below 90%, and near-hit AAI.**
  - *Code:* strains at 100, 95, 90, 85, 80, 75 and 70% to their unit's centroid (was down to 85%). On `none` rows, `near_scores` scores `aai_naive` on every hit unit that is not itself present but has a present strain in its family (a sibling paralog) against the best identity of those strains to its centroid. That is what a blanket, sylph-`query`-style AAI reports. Columns `near_bias_<bin>`, `near_cover_<bin>` (within 0.05), `near_spearman` and `near_lower_bound`.
  - *Run* (`results/sim-ladder`, 3 seeds, stop-free):

    | Config | Completeness | `aai` Spearman | `aai` − mean member at 100/90/80/70% | Coverage (centroid) |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | dense | 0.958 | 0.93 | +0.028/+0.021/+0.027/+0.041 | 0.96 |
    | floor\_d100 | 0.576 | 0.91 | +0.028/+0.021/+0.037/+0.074 | 0.94 |
    | floor\_d20 | 0.573 | 0.87 | +0.026/+0.026/+0.033/+0.076 | 0.96 |
    | s10 | 0.789 | 0.85 | +0.025/+0.024/+0.058/+0.14 | 0.96 |
    | floor | 0.576 | 0.31 | +0.03/+0.088/+0.209/+0.32 | 0.93 |

    - *`aai` holds to 70% with a dense tier.* The +0.02–0.03 offset is the consensus-vs-member offset of step 7, not divergence: it is flat from 100 to 80%. It rises at 70% only on sparse tiers, where few kept k-mers survive. The intervals stay calibrated in every config, because they widen as k-mers thin.
    - *Resolution:* `aai` separates 90% from 95–100% strains on the dense and `floor_d100` indexes (Spearman ≥ 0.9). On a 1-in-10 tier it separates them only above ~85%; on the floor alone it does not.
    - *Near hits (`aai_naive`, dense):* 87 per seed, unbiased (+0.002 at 80–90%, −0.003 at 60–80%) but noisy (76% and 64% within 0.05; Spearman 0.79). On every floored index it has 8 near hits per seed and 44% flagged as lower bounds, because `aai_naive` uses tier 2 only, whatever the dense tier. Bracketing neighbours by AAI therefore needs the dense tier for hit units, not only detected ones; recorded as a limitation, not built.

* **Phase 7, step 10 — MGnify90-level truth in the fmh benchmark: unit resolution at 90% and below, and containment AAI against alignment identity.** This replaces the divergence ladder's held-out-genome design for AAI calibration: the benchmark's genomes already sit at a range of identities to MGnify90 clusters, which DIAMOND measures directly.
  - *Truth:*
    - `MGNIFY_REPS` writes the cluster representatives of an index's members (files or the MERGE bucket directory; streamed).
    - `MGNIFY_DB` and `MGNIFY_ANNOTATE`: every genome protein is searched with `diamond blastp --sensitive`, keeping up to 25 hits at ≥ 50% identity and query coverage, in the `GENOME_PROTEINS` chunks (shared with Pfam).
    - `MGNIFY_GENES` writes per gene its clusters with identity, query and subject coverage, bitscore and rank.
    - TRUTH also writes per-gene reads and depth (`truth/seedN_genes.csv`).
  - *Scoring (`aai-score`, per MGnify index, sample and arm; `aai_summary.tsv`):*
    - detection: completeness at the 90% level (identity ≥ 0.9, both coverages ≥ 0.8); purity against nearest clusters and against any hit; recall of the nearest cluster by its identity, 95% down to 50%;
    - `aai`: bias, interval coverage and Spearman against the depth-weighted identity of the genes a detected unit is nearest to;
    - `aai_naive`: on every hit unit, near hits included, against the best identity of a present gene to it.
  - *Indexes:* `--mgnify_indexes` entries take a built index (`path`) and/or `members` (plus `pfam`, `args`) to build one in the workflow. Their profiles are profiled whatever `--labels` is, and their unit profiles kept (`PROFILE` emits `units.tsv`).
  - *Fixture:* `mini_mgnify` gives 24 clusters whose representatives are mini-genome proteins mutated to 100–70% identity (two 97% members each, Pfam labels from the genes' Pfam-mini domains, integer accessions), plus 6 unrelated clusters. In the test profile DIAMOND recovers the planted identities to ±0.01. Every cluster with a present gene at ≥ 90% is detected; nearest-cluster recall is 1.0 down to 80% and 0.75 at 70%; `aai` bias is within ±0.02 and coverage 1.0 per bin (2 seeds, a tiny fixture: plumbing, not evidence).
  - *HPC:* `ablations/aai.config` is a template for a nested MGnify subset (`run_ablations.sh aai OUTDIR`). Stub-checked: 10 seeds × 2 arms profiled and scored. Annotating against the whole release (~1.7×10⁹ representatives) is possible but ~10³–10⁴ CPU-hours, so a 1-in-100 subset comes first.
  - Tests: `mgnify-genes` keeps the best HSP per pair and ranks hits; `aai_score` on a hand-built case (detection, purity, recall below 90%, `aai` bias and coverage, near-hit `aai_naive`); per-gene truth.

* **Phase 7, step 11 — the posterior's batches in parallel.** The step 32 posterior cells (`draws` 100, ERR7738575) took 7.8 h at 40 M pairs and 10.4 h at 100 M, almost all in the posterior, on one core of the 8 the job has (`%cpu` ≈ 100). Posterior time followed detected units (5.1 → 6.9 M, ×1.33 time) more than pairs (×2.5). Taking that ratio to the pooled sample's detected units (11.3 M at 100 M, 13.7 M at 200 M; step 38) gives ~17 h and ~20 h serially, close to `QUERY`'s 24 h.
  - **Change:** `posterior_zi` runs its component batches on `workers` processes (default `pl.thread_pool_size()`, so `POLARS_MAX_THREADS` = the task's CPUs), spawned, not forked (Polars' thread pool does not survive a fork), each with one Polars thread. The index-wide arrays go to each worker once (pool initializer), at most 2 × `workers` batches' read rows are built ahead, and the largest batches go first, because a giant component's batch is the critical path. One batch or one worker runs serially, as before.
  - *Results are unchanged:* each batch already had its own generator (seed + smallest unit) and read weights keyed by read id, so the output is bit-identical to the serial run (`test_posterior_batches_in_parallel_as_in_series`).
  - *Result (synthetic, 4 K units × 100 k-mers, 16 batches, 100 draws, laptop):* 41.0 s → 6.9 s on 8 workers, identical output.
  - *Limits:* a component is still one batch on one core, so a giant one bounds the wall time (the pooled 200 M cell's largest component is 7.55 M pairs; E2 streams it but does not split it). The stage's `posterior_peak_*` are the parent's only; each worker adds about one batch (`POSTERIOR_BATCH_BYTES`, more for a giant component), which the job's trace `peak_rss` includes. Monkeypatched module constants (`READ_CHUNK`, `SWEEP_BLOCK_PAIRS`) do not reach spawned workers; the tests that set them run one batch, so serially.
  - *To read from the next posterior run:* wall time of the posterior stage against 10.4 h at 100 M pairs, and the job's peak RSS against step 38's.

* **Phase 7, step 12 — the `aai` ablation as a sparse/dense ladder at the MGnify90 level.** The tool comparison (phase 5, step 3) puts the sparse `kfp_s100` ahead of HUMAnN 3.9 and 4 on KO detection and abundance, so the dense tier is not needed for function-level profiles and stays off by default. What is still open is whether it pays at the granular level: 90%-AAI orthologue clusters and containment AAI below 90% (step 10's metrics).
  - *Change:* `ablations/aai.config` builds one nested subset (1 in 100, `cost-nested`'s published `members.parquet` and `pfam.parquet`) four times, sparse and with *t\_dense* 0.02, 0.05 and 0.1 (otherwise *k* 11, *t\_base* 0.001, *n\_min* 8), and scores all four against the same truth, plain and edges arms. Entries with the same `members` now share one DIAMOND annotation (`main.nf`), so the ladder costs one annotation, not four. The test profile builds `mgnify_mini` sparse and dense to exercise this.
  - *What the ladder can show:* detection (`completeness_90`, purity, `recall_<lo>`) and `aai_naive` come from tier-2 hits, so they are identical across the ladder (checked on the test profile). The dense tier changes `aai` (zero-inflated copies over `m_dense` k-mers), its interval (`aai_bias_<lo>`, `aai_cover_<lo>`, `aai_spearman`) and Pfam-level abundance in `summary.tsv`. The KO/Pfam-unit dense ablation (`index`: `dense05`/`10`/`20`) covers the function level.
  - *Decision rule:* ship the dense tier only if it measurably improves `aai_bias`/`aai_cover` at 0.7–0.9 identity (step 9's simulation: `aai` bias rose at 70% on sparse tiers only, with intervals still calibrated, so the expected gain is accuracy at ≤ 80%, not coverage) at a rate whose full-scale cost (step 39 of phase 6: +120 GB at 0.02, +250 GB at 0.1) is acceptable. Otherwise it stays an option. *Result: step 15.*

* **Phase 7, step 13 — first MGnify90-level run (step 10's config, one index).** `kfp-ablations/aai`, main at 0172c99: one prebuilt 1-in-100 index (`lin100-dense/1in100/index`) built with *t\_dense* 0.1; its `meta.json` has a dense layout (`max_hash` 0.2 × 2⁶⁴: the layout spans the units' max(*t\_dense*, *t\_g*), up to *t\_cap* 0.2), so the query used the dense tier, 10 seeds × 2 arms, 2 h 37 min. Not the step 12 ladder, which still needs running.
  - *Truth:* 2.78 M benchmark genes searched; nearest-cluster identity is mostly 50–80% (1.59 M at 50–70%, 0.60 M at 70–80%, 0.39 M at 80–90%, 0.16 M ≥ 90%). Per sample, ~110 k genes present, 85 k with a hit, only 3.5 k (3%) in a cluster at the 90% level, as expected for 1 in 100 clusters. 1.24 M genes hit the 25-target cap, so hits beyond the 25th are missing: `purity_near` is a lower bound.
  - *Results (mean of 10 seeds; plain / edges20; sd across seeds ≤ 0.03 throughout):*

    | Metric | ≥ 95% | 90–95% | 80–90% | 70–80% | 50–70% |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | `recall` (nearest cluster detected) | 0.39 / 0.40 | 0.33 / 0.34 | 0.27 / 0.28 | 0.18 / 0.19 | 0.08 / 0.09 |
    | `aai` bias | +0.006 / +0.007 | +0.050 / +0.051 | +0.081 / +0.081 | +0.134 / +0.135 | +0.226 / +0.226 |
    | `aai` interval coverage | 0.82 / 0.80 | 0.55 / 0.53 | 0.42 / 0.40 | 0.19 / 0.18 | 0.04 / 0.03 |
    | `aai_naive` bias | +0.006 / +0.007 | +0.046 / +0.048 | +0.069 / +0.070 | +0.131 / +0.131 | +0.275 / +0.275 |
    | `aai_naive` within 0.05 | 0.89 / 0.91 | 0.39 / 0.39 | 0.39 / 0.39 | 0.05 / 0.05 | 0.00 / 0.00 |

    `completeness_90` 0.336 / 0.344; `purity_nearest` 0.15 / 0.15, `purity_near` 0.32 / 0.31; `aai` on 5.8 k / 6.1 k units per sample (2.4 k nearest to several genes), Spearman 0.61 / 0.61; `aai_naive` on 19 k / 20 k units, Spearman 0.34 / 0.35. Pfam level (`coverage_zi`, min hits 1): completeness 0.74 / 0.75, weighted 0.98 / 0.98, purity 0.74 / 0.73, Spearman 0.82 / 0.83, L1 0.49 / 0.48.
  - *Gate (Sequence similarity): fails.* Bias is within 0.01 only at ≥ 95%; +0.05 at 90–95%. Coverage 0.82 at ≥ 95% and falls with identity; Spearman 0.61 against ≥ 0.9.
  - *Reading:*
    - The bias grows steadily as identity falls, and the intervals do not widen to match it. On the simulation ladder (step 9) the dense index's bias was flat (+0.02–0.03 from 100 to 70%) and this pattern belonged to the sparse tiers (`s10`: +0.058 at 80%, +0.14 at 70%; floor: +0.21, +0.32). This index has a dense tier at 0.1, so the dense tier's accuracy in simulation does not carry over to real divergence: the cause is in the estimator or the truth, not in too few k-mers. Candidates: clustered variation (real substitutions concentrate in variable regions, which simulation mutated uniformly, so more k-mers survive than *a*^k predicts: Sequence similarity, bias 1); the union mosaic, larger in real clusters with diverse members; and selection, since below 80% a unit is only detected when a conserved patch survives (recall 0.18 at 70–80%), which conditions on high containment. The intervals model only sampling noise, so a systematic bias of this size falls outside them.
    - Part of the +0.006 at ≥ 95% and of the offset below is the truth's own offset: DIAMOND identity is to the representative, `aai` to a typical member (+0.025 to 97% members in step 7).
    - Unit-level detection is low: a third of the 90%-level clusters with a present gene are detected, and 39% even at ≥ 95%, at median gene depth 2.6×. Detection uses tier 2 only, so a dense tier cannot change it. Most detected units (68%) are hit by no present gene within the truth's 25 targets; how many of those are real false positives, and how many are cap losses or shared 11-mers, the run cannot tell.
    - Below 70% neither estimator measures anything (0.6^11 ≈ 0.004 of k-mers survive): read `aai` as informative above ~0.9 only, even with the dense tier.
    - `--frames edges:20` adds ~1 point of recall and completeness at every identity, costs ~1 point of purity, and leaves `aai` unchanged: the gene-end loss it removes is small next to the bias above.
  - *Defect found:* `copies_zi` is `inf` where `pin_sum` is 0 (`query.py`, `copies = present · m / pin_sum`), so `abundance_zi` is `inf` (51 Pfam rows over 20 profiles) and its L1 is NaN in `summary.tsv`. `aai` of such a unit is capped to 1. *Cause:* `p_in` is 0 for k-mers seen only in fragment members (it counts full-length members), so a unit whose kept k-mers are all such has `pin_sum` 0; a unit with some has a `pin_sum` too small, and inflated copies. *Fixed* in the build: `p_in` = max(*c*, 0.5) / `n_counting` (`P_IN`, `index.py`, shared by the single and partitioned builds, tier 2 and dense), the half-member weight the score already gave these k-mers, so postings and scores are unchanged and only `p_in`, `pin_q` and `pin_sum` move. Indexes built before this keep the defect until rebuilt (the step 12 ladder builds its own). Test: `test_copies_finite_when_kept_kmers_are_fragment_only`.
  - *Next:* run the step 12 ladder (sparse, *t\_dense* 0.02/0.05/0.1 on the same subset): its sparse arm measures what the dense tier gains on real data at all. If the bias stays monotone in identity on every dense rate, a calibration fitted on this truth (Sequence similarity, bias 1: a monotone correction per alphabet and *k*, stored in the index metadata) is the fix to try, with intervals widened by its residual spread. The unit profiles (`units.tsv`) were published to the same path as the Pfam profile and overwritten; now under `units/`, so bias can be stratified by kept k-mers and depth. The ladder's indexes can also be built ahead in `mgnify-subset` (`--members`/`--pfam_table` from `cost-nested`, `--t_dense 0,0.02,0.05,0.1`: one INDEX job per rate) and given to `aai.config` as `path`.

* **Phase 7, step 14 — unknown fraction of a metagenome (in code; evaluation pending).** A sample-level "how much of this metagenome do the units explain", as sylph's `--estimate-unknown` gives for genomes. Dropped k-mers do not prevent it: sylph samples too, and converts each detected genome's coverage into bases (coverage × genome length / sample bases) instead of counting k-mer hits. Here `coverage_zi` is already depth per kept k-mer, fitted against the recorded kept set and *p\_in*, so the k-mers the build drops (hash sampling, floor trimming, promiscuity cut, adapter mask) do not bias it. Two estimates, reported side by side because they answer different questions:
  - **A. Model-based (`explained_fraction`, the sylph analogue).** explained = Σ\_g λ̂\_g · ρ · ℓ̄\_g · 3 / (*r* · *B*), unknown = 1 − explained.
    - λ̂\_g = `coverage_zi`: depth over present k-mers, so a divergent variant still counts at its full depth. Not `abundance_zi`, which multiplies by the present fraction and undercounts divergent units.
    - ρ = *L* / (*L* − *k* + 1), k-mer depth to base depth, in amino acids (1.25 for 150 bp reads at *k* = 11).
    - ℓ̄\_g: mean member length in amino acids (counting members). **Not in the unit table:** `n_kmers` is the members' union (inflated by diversity) and `pin_sum` / *t\_g* is biased because floored units keep their highest-*p\_in* k-mers. New build column `len_mean`, a group-by over the members table (single and partitioned builds).
    - *r*: error thinning (~12% of 33-nt windows carry an error at 0.5% per base), from read quality scores (Error vs variation model, "Absolute coverage").
    - *B*: total read bases.
    - Interval from the posterior draws of `coverage_zi` when `--draws` > 0 (not built: the posterior returns per-unit intervals, not joint draws, so a sum's interval needs the draws kept).
    - Biases: units below detection count as unknown, so the unknown fraction runs high in diverse samples (as in sylph); the planned partial fix (adding undetected hit units at λ = hits / `pin_sum`) has nothing to add: gather keeps every unit with an own k-mer, so a hit unit it drops has all its hits explained by kept units, and adding them would count those hits twice. Non-coding reads (~12% of a bacterial genome, rRNA, host) count as unknown; report the raw value, no coding-density normalisation. Strain mixtures inflate λ̂ and so the explained fraction.
  - **B. Model-free census (`census_containment`).** The fraction of sampled query k-mers with hash ≤ *t\_base* found in the index: includes rare, undetected units and needs no model; ~4×10⁵ census k-mers per Gbp at *t\_base* = 1/1000.
    - *Needs a complete base stratum.* Floored units keep only their `n_min` best-scoring candidates (`index.py`, the postings filter), so some of their k-mers below *t\_base* are dropped and sample k-mers from those proteins look novel. Change: also keep hash ≤ max\_hash(*t\_base*) for floored units, ~0.1 extra posting per floored unit (~1–2% of postings); A is unaffected because the kept set is recorded. Adapter-masked and promiscuous k-mers still miss (≤ 0.5% of candidates); count them as known.
    - *k-mer fraction ≠ read fraction.* Off-frame k-mers never hit (~1.5 frames kept per coding read, ~2.9 at 70% GC): count only reads with exactly one stop-free frame per mate, whose frame is the coding one if the read is coding (~62% of coding reads at moderate GC). Weight by occurrences (≈ reads) and correct by *r* for error k-mers.
    - *Divergence horizon.* Exact 11-mers hit at ≳ 80% AAI, so B is "unknown at ~80% AAI", while A is "unknown beyond the detection band" (~75–85%, step 13).
  - *Evaluation:* the fmh benchmark with genomes held out of the index (0, 10, 30, 50% of the 64), truth = fraction of read bases from held-out genes (and from non-coding sequence, reported separately); then the simulation's divergence ladder (step 9) for unknown fraction against identity.
  - *Decision rule:* ship A as a summary line of the profile if it is within 0.05 of truth across the hold-out ladder after the *r* correction; ship B if, with the complete base stratum, it tracks truth monotonically (Spearman ≥ 0.9 across the ladder). Genome mode's "unexplained fraction" (phase 11) is a different, genome-level quantity and does not replace either.
  - *Implemented:*
    - *Reads:* `FastxHits.base_stats` (Rust reader thread): bases, `lost` (not A/C/G/T after the quality mask) and `expected_errors` (summed Phred error probabilities of the others; 0 for FASTA). *r* = (1 − (lost + 0.75 · expected\_errors) / bases)^(3*k*), with 0.75 the share of substitutions that change the amino acid (`SENSE_CHANGE`). Errors clustered in read tails make *r* too low and the explained fraction too high; reduced alphabets would need a larger synonymous share.
    - *Build:* `len_mean` (mean length of counting members) in the unit table, single and partitioned builds. Floored units also keep every candidate with hash ≤ *t\_base*, and `meta.json` records `base_stratum_complete`; indexes built before this have no census. On the cost test subset, *k* = 6, *t\_base* = 0.01: 5,566 postings, about 30% more than the old rule's (whose prediction, 4,255, was within 5% of its build) (at the full release's *t\_base* = 0.001 and median 114 k-mers per cluster, ~0.1 posting per floored unit). `predict_cost` adds the base stratum with a random overlap with the *n\_min* best, an upper bound (5,992 predicted): score ties go to the lowest hash, so the base stratum is often among the *n\_min* already.
    - *Query:* `profile(summary=dict)` and `query --summary PATH` (JSON) fill `sample_summary`: `explained_fraction`, `unknown_fraction` (floored at 0), `explained_bases`, `error_thinning`, `census_kmers`, `census_hits`, `census_containment` (÷ *r*, capped at 1), `census_unknown`, `census_frame_miss` and the base counts. `--summary` fits ZI. ρ uses the mean whole codons per frame, (*L* − 2)/3, not *L*/3. One-frame mates are found from the sampled k-mers at *t\_max*, so a kept frame with none sampled is missed: `census_frame_miss` = (1 − *t\_max*)^windows, ~10⁻⁴ at *t\_max* = 0.2 and 150 bp. The census needs the kfp hash (sourmash-hashed queries have no frames) and the minimum *t\_base* over joint indexes; host-masked k-mers are left out of both counts.
    - *Tests:* by-hand values of `sample_summary`; floored units keep their whole base stratum; `len_mean` over counting members only. End to end (*k* = 7, *t\_base* = 0.05, five 400-codon genes, error-free 150 bp reads of either strand): half known, half random reads → `explained_fraction` 0.54; all known → 1.09 and `census_containment` 0.985; all random → no detected unit and census 0.0. The ~9% excess on known reads is partly the test's own: its reads never cross gene ends, so end k-mers are thinly covered and ZI takes them as absent. In real data, reads crossing gene ends exist but the stop-free filter drops them, so the interior depth that ZI fits is the true one. The census of the mixed sample is 0.61, not 0.5: it counts k-mers in one-frame mates, and known reads have more of those. So B tracks the known fraction without equalling it, which is what its decision rule allows for.
  - *Next:* the genome hold-out ladder in `workflows/fmh-benchmark` (index without 0/10/30/50% of the 64 genomes; truth = read bases from held-out genes and from non-coding sequence), run with `--summary`; then the step 9 ladder.

* **Phase 7, step 15 — the step 12 ladder on HPC.** `kfp-ablations/aai`, main at dc4a13e (after the `p_in` fix of step 13): four indexes on the same 1-in-100 subset, prebuilt in `mgnify-subset` (`lin100-dense-ladder`: sparse and *t\_dense* 0.02, 0.05, 0.1), one shared DIAMOND annotation, 10 seeds × 2 arms, 2 h 18 min. The outdir also holds step 13's stale `*_mgnify_1in100` files (its 11:48 run; the 26 `inf` rows found are all in those); this run's profiles have none and `summary.tsv` has no NaN, so the `p_in` fix holds.
  - *Detection is identical across the ladder*, as step 12 predicted (tier 2 only): `completeness_90` 0.336 / 0.344 (plain / edges20), `recall` 0.39 → 0.08 from ≥ 95% to 50–70%, `aai_naive` unchanged. Pfam level is the same too (`kmers_unique`, min hits 1: completeness 0.737, purity 0.742, Spearman 0.79 sparse vs 0.79 dense); L1 is slightly worse with a dense tier (0.554 sparse, 0.570–0.573 dense, plain arm).
  - *`aai` (plain arm, mean of 10 seeds; edges20 within 0.01 of these throughout):*

    | Index | `aai_n` | Spearman | bias ≥ 95% | 90–95% | 80–90% | 70–80% | 50–70% | cover ≥ 95% | 90–95% | 80–90% | 70–80% |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | sparse | 3.1 k | −0.18 | +0.008 | +0.062 | +0.129 | +0.232 | +0.361 | 0.94 | 0.75 | 0.65 | 0.64 |
    | *t\_dense* 0.02 | 5.0 k | 0.53 | +0.005 | +0.047 | +0.082 | +0.143 | +0.244 | 0.87 | 0.65 | 0.48 | 0.20 |
    | *t\_dense* 0.05 | 5.3 k | 0.56 | +0.004 | +0.044 | +0.078 | +0.138 | +0.237 | 0.85 | 0.63 | 0.48 | 0.20 |
    | *t\_dense* 0.1 | 5.8 k | 0.61 | +0.004 | +0.042 | +0.074 | +0.129 | +0.221 | 0.82 | 0.60 | 0.46 | 0.21 |

    Step 13's index (*t\_dense* 0.1, before the `p_in` fix) had +0.050 / +0.081 / +0.134 and coverage 0.55 / 0.42 / 0.19 at 90–95 / 80–90 / 70–80%: the fix gained ~0.005–0.008 of bias and ~0.04 of coverage.
  - *Reading:*
    - *Sparse `aai` measures nothing.* Every unit keeps *n\_min* = 8 tier-2 k-mers (median `m_g` 8), so `copies` is a handful of present-or-absent k-mers and saturates: the median sparse `aai` is 1.0 in every identity bin, and 54–85% of units are at 1.0 below 90%. Its high coverage comes from intervals 0.74 wide below 80%, not from accuracy; its Spearman is negative. It is also null on more units (56% of detected units with truth, against 30% at 0.02 and 18% at 0.1: fewer than `MIN_AAI_KMERS` present).
    - *The rate barely matters because the floor sets it.* The dense threshold is max(*t\_dense*, *t\_g*), and on this subset the median *t\_g* is 0.074 (median union 268 k-mers, floored to 8 by score, so *t\_g* reaches past the first 8 by hash). So 0.02 and 0.05 mostly reproduce the floor's own threshold (median `m_dense` 24 and 26 over all units, seed 1); only 0.1 goes past it for most units (31, 90th percentile 79). The ladder therefore compares "every k-mer under *t\_g*" against "every k-mer under 0.1", and the second adds little: −0.008 of bias at 80–90%, +0.08 Spearman, +16% units with an `aai`.
    - *The bias is systematic, not sampling.* On *t\_dense* 0.1 the bias in each identity bin is flat in gene depth (80–90%: +0.067 at ≤ 2×, +0.087 at > 10×; 70–80%: +0.122 to +0.142), while the intervals narrow with depth, so coverage falls with depth (80–90%: 0.70 at ≤ 2×, 0.25 at > 10×). More k-mers or reads do not remove it; it is in the estimator or the truth (step 13's candidates: clustered variation, the union mosaic, selection on conserved patches, and the representative-vs-member offset of the truth).
  - *Decision (step 12's rule):* the dense tier is what makes `aai` an estimate at all at the cluster level (sparse is saturated), and its gain is in bias at 70–90% (+0.129 → +0.082 at 80–90%, +0.232 → +0.143 at 70–80%) and in ranking (Spearman −0.18 → 0.53). That gain comes with the lowest rung: 0.02 is within 0.01 of 0.1 everywhere. So if granular `aai` ships, it ships with *t\_dense* 0.02 (or a raised floor in tier 2 instead, step 19) (+120 GB at full scale, step 39 of phase 6; less if most units' *t\_g* already exceed it there too, worth checking in `predict_cost`). The function-level profile does not need it (Pfam metrics equal, L1 slightly worse). The Sequence similarity gate still fails on every rung (bias within 0.01 only at ≥ 95%, Spearman ≤ 0.61), so the dense tier stays an option and `aai` stays labelled informative above ~0.9 only.
  - *Next:* the calibration step 13 proposed. A monotone map from `aai` to identity (isotonic fit of truth on `aai`, per alphabet and *k*, stored in the index metadata), fitted on half the seeds of this run and scored on the other half, plus a model-error term in the interval sized from the residual spread, since the bias does not shrink with depth and the sampling-only interval does. Fit it on *t\_dense* 0.02, the rung that would ship.

* **Phase 7, step 16 — inverse calibration of `aai` (`bench.py aai-calibrate`).** Step 15's proposed fix, fitted and scored on its ladder outputs (no new HPC run).
  - *Method:* `fit_aai_calibration` bins units by true identity (0.01), takes the median `aai` per bin (≥ 20 units), makes it non-decreasing (pool adjacent violators, `isotonic`) and inverts it: `calibrate_aai` maps `aai`, `aai_lo` and `aai_hi` through g⁻¹, linear between knots, and widens the interval by `widen`, the 95% quantile (over units at ≥ 70% identity) of how far the truth falls outside the mapped interval. `aai-calibrate` fits on half the clusters (by a hash of `cluster_rep`: a nested subset keeps every 100th accession, so parity would put them all in one half) of the 10 samples and scores raw and calibrated `aai` with `aai_score` on the other half. Genes differ across seeds (1.9 k of 111 k shared), clusters do not (45% recur), so a seed split would leak.
  - *Why inverse:* regressing identity on `aai` (isotonic, tried first) is calibrated given the estimate but pulls every estimate toward the benchmark's mean identity (most units are 50–80%): held out, it moved the bias at ≥ 95% from +0.005 to −0.12 and at 90–95% from +0.047 to −0.075, and it would put the benchmark's identity distribution into the index. Inverting g(a) = median `aai` at identity a conditions on the truth, so the map depends only on how `aai` responds to identity.
  - *The map (t\_dense 0.02, plain):* `aai` 0.873 → 0.55, 0.886 → 0.725, 0.911 → 0.805, 0.945 → 0.885, 0.970 → 0.925, 1.0 → 1.0; `widen` 0.059 (0.085 at 0.1). `aai` spans only 0.87–0.90 for identity 0.55–0.77: below ~0.8 it hardly moves.
  - *Results (held-out clusters, mean of 10 samples; raw → calibrated; edges20 within 0.01):*

    | Index | Spearman | bias ≥ 95% | 90–95% | 80–90% | 70–80% | 50–70% | cover ≥ 95% | 90–95% | 80–90% | 70–80% | 50–70% |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | *t\_dense* 0.02 | 0.53 → 0.53 | +0.005 → −0.004 | +0.047 → +0.002 | +0.083 → +0.006 | +0.142 → −0.003 | +0.245 → +0.040 | 0.86 → 0.99 | 0.66 → 0.99 | 0.48 → 0.95 | 0.20 → 0.93 | 0.06 → 0.88 |
    | *t\_dense* 0.1 | 0.61 → 0.60 | +0.004 → −0.003 | +0.043 → +0.003 | +0.075 → +0.002 | +0.128 → −0.006 | +0.222 → +0.017 | 0.80 → 0.99 | 0.59 → 0.99 | 0.45 → 0.95 | 0.21 → 0.93 | 0.04 → 0.90 |

  - *Reading:*
    - The bias half of the Sequence similarity gate now passes on held-out clusters: within ±0.01 from 70 to 100% on both rungs and arms, so the representative-vs-member offset and the real-divergence bias of steps 13 and 15 are both absorbed. Intervals are calibrated (≥ 0.93 at ≥ 70%), but wide: in the scratch evaluation before `widen` was fitted per level, the calibrated 95% intervals were ~0.26 wide at ≥ 95% and ~0.35–0.46 below, about four times the raw intervals.
    - The ranking half cannot pass this way: a monotone map leaves Spearman at 0.53 / 0.61 (gate ≥ 0.9). Per unit the calibrated error is large (median absolute error 0.025 at ≥ 95%, ~0.06 at 90–95%, 0.065–0.08 at 80–90%, 0.10–0.135 at 70–80%; from the scratch evaluation), and the map is flat below ~0.8, so `aai` separates ≥ 95% from 90% and 90% from 80%, but not much below that. The limit is per-unit scatter that does not shrink with depth (step 15), so more reads do not help; it is in what `aai` measures (containment of a cluster's union at *k* = 11) against what DIAMOND measures (identity of one gene to the representative).
    - *Transfer:* the map is fitted on 64 KEGG genomes against a 1-in-100 subset at *k* 11, *t\_base* 0.001, *n\_min* 8. The dense threshold per unit is the same rule at full scale, so the map should carry over to a full-release index with the same parameters, but it depends on *t\_dense* (0.02 and 0.1 differ) and would need refitting for another alphabet, *k* or rate. A full-release check needs a truth annotation against the whole release (step 10: ~10³–10⁴ CPU-hours), or a 1-in-10 subset as a step towards it.
  - *Not built yet:* storing the map in the index and applying it in `query`. Built in step 17.
  - Test: `test_aai_calibration_inverts_a_biased_estimator` (a compressed, biased, noisy estimator over 70–100% is recovered to within 0.005 median bias, ≥ 94% coverage; null `aai` stays null).

* **Phase 7, step 17 — the `aai` calibration in the index and the query.**
  - *Index:* a map is a sidecar, `aai_calibration.json` in the index directory, attached after the build with `kmer-functional-profiler calibrate-aai INDEX_DIR MAP.json` (no map argument removes it) and read by `Index.load` (`Index.aai_calibration`). *Amendment:* step 13 said "stored in the index metadata"; a sidecar keeps `meta.json` as built, so the SHA-256 a mask records of it (`mask.json`) stays true, and a map can be refitted or removed without rebuilding.
  - *Check on attach* (`check_aai_calibration`): `aai` knots strictly increasing, `identity` non-decreasing in [0, 1], `widen` ≥ 0, and the build parameters the map records equal to the index's in `AAI_CALIBRATION_PARAMS` (*k*, alphabet, *t\_base*, *n\_min*, *t\_cap*, *t\_dense*; step 16 found the map depends on *t\_dense*). `aai-calibrate --index DIR` records them; a map without them is not checked.
  - *Query:* `profile` maps `aai`, `aai_lo`, `aai_hi` through each index's map (`calibrate_aai`, moved from `bench.py`, which now imports it), on that index's own units with `--extra-index` (`source`), and keeps the raw estimate as `aai_raw`, `aai_raw_lo`, `aai_raw_hi`. Indexes without a map report raw `aai` as before. `aai-calibrate` refits a calibrated index's profiles on their raw columns.
  - *Workflow:* `AAI_CALIBRATE` fits and scores a map per MGnify index and arm over all seeds (`calibration/<name>[~arm].json`, `_scores.tsv`), so every fmh-benchmark run with `--mgnify_indexes` produces one. A fit with fewer than two knots (the test fixture's 24 clusters) writes an empty map (`widen` null), which `calibrate-aai` refuses.
  - *Requirement before a map is attached to a released index: test the calibration over a broader range of benchmarks.* Step 16's map is fitted and scored on one benchmark: 64 KEGG genomes, simulated NovaSeq reads at one depth distribution, a 1-in-100 MGnify subset, one *k* and alphabet. Its held-out score splits clusters, not benchmarks, so it does not show that the map transfers. To test:
    - *Genome sets:* other taxa and GC ranges than the KEGG 64 (e.g. CAMI2 genomes, MGnify MAGs from several biomes, whose proteins are the population MGnify clusters were built from), so the identity-to-`aai` response is not one collection's.
    - *Subset density:* 1-in-10 and, once its truth annotation is affordable, the full release. Cluster sizes and the floor's *t\_g* set the dense threshold (step 15), and they change with the subset.
    - *Reads:* depth (the bias was flat in depth, step 15, but the interval and `widen` are not), read length, error profile (`--iss_mode`, HiSeq/MiSeq models), and the query arms (frames, quality mask).
    - *Controlled divergence:* step 9's simulation ladder, where identity is set rather than measured, separates the map from DIAMOND's representative-vs-member offset.
    - *Real samples:* a mock community with known genomes.
    - *Criterion:* fit on one benchmark, score on another. The map ships only if, transferred, bias stays within ±0.01 at 70–100% and coverage ≥ 0.9 in every bin there. If it does not transfer, the variable it depends on (genome set, density, depth) becomes an input to the map or a reason not to ship it. Until then the release index carries no map and `aai` stays raw, labelled informative above ~0.9.
  - Tests: `test_aai_calibration_attached_and_applied_per_index` (CLI refuses a map fitted at another *k*; attached to one of two joint indexes, it maps that index's units only and keeps `aai_raw`; constant below the first knot; removal restores raw `aai`); step 16's fit test.

* **Phase 7, step 18 — step 38's posterior cells: ERR7738575 + ERR7746321 pooled at 100 and 200 M pairs, `draws` 100 (HPC, `full-build`, scratch copy + preload, 8 CPUs, step 37's code: the posterior still serial, before step 11).** Both cells completed, inside `QUERY`'s 24 h. The `draws` 0 cells in the same output are step 38's (identical files). **200 M pairs pooled with intervals: 21.5 h query wall (21.3 h of it posterior), 35.2 GB peak anonymous, 105 GB peak RSS (stats; the trace's 96.6 GB).**

  | Pooled, pairs | read rows | lookup (s) | fit\_zi (s) | posterior (h) | posterior / detected unit (ms) | total (h) | peak anon, `draws` 0 → 100 (GB) | posterior stage peak anon (GB) | trace peak RSS, `draws` 0 → 100 (GB) |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 100 M | 348 M | 178.4 | 57.1 | 17.33 | 5.54 | 17.47 | 10.07 → 24.59 | 18.45 | 79.9 → 87.2 |
  | 200 M | 695 M | 195.8 | 65.2 | 21.30 | 5.59 | 21.47 | 11.89 → 35.19 | 27.73 | 81.8 → 96.6 |

  - **Posterior time is linear in detected units**, at 5.5–5.6 ms each (step 36's single 100 M cell: 10.4 h for 6.9 M units, 5.4 ms). Step 11's serial estimates (~17 h and ~20 h) held to within 7%. CPU/wall is 1.01: one core of 8, as expected before step 11.
  - **E2 was not exercised.** The detected units' largest components (4.1 K and 18.6 K units, step 38) are tiny against 11.3–13.7 M detected units, so no batch was large. The posterior stage itself added 1.7 GB (100 M) and 1.2 GB (200 M) over `fit_zi`'s peak. The same small components mean step 11's parallel batches are not bounded by a giant one: ~8× on 8 workers would put the 200 M cell at ~3 h (an estimate; not measured).
  - **The per-read rows set the memory.** `draws` > 0 keeps a (hash, read) row per hit read k-mer: 7.0 GB at 100 M and 14.2 GB at 200 M, both 20 B per row, held from lookup to the end. The other increment is untimed: total peak anon exceeds the posterior stage's by 6.1 GB (100 M) and 7.5 GB (200 M), both 545 B per detected unit, so it is the result assembly after the posterior (the join of the intervals, `fill_null`, sort) while the per-read rows are still alive. With `draws` 0 that assembly stays under `presence`'s peak.
  - *Against the targets:* 35.2 GB is within the gate's 64 GB but not the 12 GB laptop target. Intervals are opt-in (`--draws` 0 by default, phase 7 step 8), so the laptop target stays a `draws` 0 one; for intervals at depth on a laptop, `--low-memory` (step 29) is the route, and is unmeasured at this depth.
  - *Lookup* took 178 s against the `draws` 0 cell's 129 s at 100 M, and 196 against 207 s at 200 M: the job-to-job variance of step 38 again, not a `draws` effect.
  - *Not in this output:* the `COPIES_ERROR` re-check (it needs profiles scored against truth, not cost stats). Still pending.
  - **Next:** none for the draws. Project-wide, 2026-10-02: confidence comes from closed-form estimates on the k-mers, so this run is the last of the posterior cells, and the planned re-run on step 11's code, the per-read-row memory cut and the `COPIES_ERROR` re-check are dropped.

* **Phase 7, step 19 — the ablation batch, revised after steps 15–18, and a raised floor tested as a replacement for the dense tier (set up, not run).** Step 6's runs had not been launched. This step updates them for steps 15–18 and adds one question: can a raised density floor in tier 2 replace the dense tier?
  - *What steps 15–18 change in the batch:*
    - *Draws dropped (step 18, project-wide).* The fmh benchmark's `--draws` default is now 0. `ci_cover`, `ci_width` and `group_size_mean` stay empty until the closed-form intervals and point-fit ambiguity groups exist, so they are not decision metrics in this batch. Profiles cost a fraction of what they did (draws were 5–10× the rest, Query cost).
    - *The `aai` ladder has run (step 15).* Its run leaves the batch. `aai.config` stays as its record; `floor.config` keeps its sparse and *t\_dense* 0.02 rungs as controls.
    - *Calibration transfer (step 17).* The new MGnify90-level runs are repeated per read model and depth, so a map fitted on one run can be scored on another. `bench.py aai-calibrate --map MAP.json` scores a given map on the held-out half of the clusters (the same hash split as the fit, so no cluster is in both) instead of fitting one, and refuses a map whose recorded build parameters differ from `--index`'s (`check_aai_calibration`). Test: `test_aai_calibrate_transfers_a_map_between_runs` (a map fitted on one run removes a 0.15 bias on another's held-out clusters to within 0.01 with coverage ≥ 0.9; a map from a *k* 9 index is refused).
  - **Raised floor instead of the dense tier: the hypothesis.** Step 15 found that dense 0.02 does what dense 0.1 does, because the dense threshold max(*t\_dense*, *t\_g*) is mostly the floor's own *t\_g* (median 0.074 on the 1-in-100 subset). So the dense tier's gain is mainly the floored units' *unpruned* k-mers up to *t\_g*, which tier 2 cuts to the *n\_min* = 8 best by score. *t\_base* is already a density floor: since step 14 every unit keeps all its k-mers with hash ≤ *t\_base* (the base stratum), so `--t-base 0.02` is "a raised floor at 0.02" with no new code. Two variants:
    - `tbase02` (`--t-base 0.02 --n-min 8`): everything up to 0.02, plus the 8 best up to *t\_g*. Fewer k-mers than dense 0.02 for floored units.
    - `tbase02_all` (`--t-base 0.02 --n-min 32 --oversample 1`): the same *t\_g* as the default (32 / *n\_kmers*: *n\_min* 8 at oversample 4), with about every candidate kept instead of the best 8, so tier 2 holds about dense 0.02's set. On the MGnify fixture (*k* 6) it keeps 889 k-mers against dense 0.02's 937 (95%; the rest are units with more than 32 candidates by chance, cut to the best 32) and plain `tbase02` 312, with identical *t\_g*.
    - *What would be simpler:* no fourth build pass, dense table or `m_dense`/`pin_*_dense`/`len_cv_dense` columns, no `--t-dense`, no second query pass. Since the draws went, that pass is the only reason the query keeps read ids. `aai` and abundance would use one k-mer set.
    - *What can differ:* dense k-mers are probed only for units already detected, so they cannot cause a detection. Floor k-mers count in pass 1: detection, gather, EM, purity and host hits. That could raise unit-level completeness (0.34 at the 90% level, step 13) or lower purity. Pass 1 gets ~20× more base-stratum hits per read (0.02 against 0.001), so the hit tables and lookup grow; hashing does not, since the query already samples at *t\_max* = *t\_cap*. Singletons get the 0.02 floor too, as they get the dense tier now.
    - `tbase02_all` against `dense02` tests where the k-mers sit; `tbase02` against `tbase02_all` tests whether the plain floor is enough.
  - **Runs** (README, Ablations; `run_ablations.sh <config> OUTDIR`, each its own outdir and work directory; the shared databases fetched first with `run_hpc.sh --dbs_only --dbs pfam,hostile,host,decoy`, so parallel runs do not download into one `storeDir` at once):

    | Run | Arms | What it decides | Profiles |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | `floor` × 5 (new; `--aai_subset` = step 15's 1-in-100 subset) | MGnify90 level, one DIAMOND truth per run: `sparse` (*k* 11, *t\_base* 0.001, *n\_min* 8), `dense02`, `tbase01`/`02`/`05`, `tbase02_all`; plain and edges20; Pfam label. Runs: NovaSeq, MiSeq (`--iss_model miseq`), error-free (`--iss_mode perfect`), 1.65 M and 26.4 M reads (`--n_reads`) | Floor against dense on `aai` (raw and calibrated), detection and Pfam; robustness across error model and depth; the calibration's transfer (step 17's read axes) for every index. Indexes published as `mgnify_index/<name>/index` | 6 × 2 × 10 per run |
    | `index` (updated) | KO and Pfam units: base; *n\_min* 0/4/16; *t\_base* 0.01/0.02/0.05 and `tbase02_all`; *t\_dense* 0.02/0.05/0.1 (0.2 dropped); *t\_cap* 0.05/0.1 (new: in phase 6's tuning list, missing from step 6); *k* 9/10/12; Murphy-10 *k* 13/15; Dayhoff *k* 17/20 | Function-level defaults, one factor at a time. For KO/Pfam units *t\_g* ≈ *t\_base*, so `tbase02` against `dense02` holds nearly the same k-mers and isolates pass 1 against pass 2 | 41 indexes × 10 |
    | `reads` NovaSeq, MiSeq | As step 6, with `tbase02` replacing *t\_base* 0.01 as the second index | Frame mode and read QC, also on the floor candidate | 5 × 11 × 10 each |
    | `host` | As step 6, plus a `tbase02` index | Ships the human mask or not; and whether the floor lets host reads cause detections, which the dense tier cannot | 4 shares × 5 arms × 5 indexes × 10, less the arms unbuilt indexes lack |
    | Query cost (new; after `floor` NovaSeq) | `mgnify-subset --query` on the six published indexes; ERR7738575 + ERR7746321 pooled at 4 M, 40 M and 100 M pairs; draws 0 | Time and peak memory, floor against dense | 18 queries |

    Step 6's `reads`-error-free and depth variants are covered by the `floor` runs and not run separately. The step 14 genome hold-out ladder needs new code and is not in this batch.
  - **Decision rule.** Ship the simplest arm that passes, in the order `tbase02`, `tbase02_all`, then `dense02` (a dense tier is kept only if no floor passes), if in the NovaSeq `floor` run and held in the other four:
    1. *`aai`*, raw and calibrated: bias at 70–90% within 0.01 of `dense02`'s; Spearman ≥ `dense02`'s − 0.03; at least as many units with an `aai` (`aai_n`).
    2. *Detection:* `completeness_90` and `recall_<lo>` ≥ `sparse`'s; `purity_nearest` and `purity_near` ≥ `sparse`'s − 0.01.
    3. *Pfam* (`floor`, and `index` against base): purity, completeness, L1 and Spearman within 0.01 of the sparse control.
    4. *Host* (`host`): false detections and `host_like_detected` at 90 and 99% host no higher than base's.
    5. *Cost:* index size ≤ `dense02`'s at 1 in 100; query time and peak memory at 100 M pairs ≤ `dense02`'s.
    - If a floor passes, the dense tier (build pass 4, dense table, query pass 2, `--t-dense`) goes, and Index encoding, Query cost and the phase-6 cost model are rewritten for one tier. Its full-scale size comes from the whole-release statistics (`--cost_args` with `--t-base 0.001 0.01 0.02 0.05 --t-dense 0 0.02`) when that pass has run.
    - *Calibration transfer:* for the winning index, a map fitted on the NovaSeq run and scored with `--map` on each other run keeps bias within ±0.01 at 70–100% and coverage ≥ 0.9 (step 17's criterion, for the read axes; genome sets and subset density remain).
  - *Workflow changes:* `ablations/floor.config` (new); `index.config`, `reads.config` and `host.config` as above; `MGNIFY_INDEX` publishes its index (hard links, so the work directory must share the outdir's filesystem, as `run_ablations.sh`'s does); `run_ablations.sh` accepts `floor`.
  - *Checked:* stub runs of `floor`, `index`, `reads` and `host` on the test profile (with a resource cap for the laptop: the HPC overrides ask for 32–64 GB) give 24, 82 (+4 from the test profile's own MGnify indexes), 154 and 208 profiles over 2 seeds. `floor` publishes all six indexes and a map per index and arm.
  - *Run directories:* `hpc/kfp-ablations/`, one per run (`00-dbs`, five `floor`, `index`, two `reads`, `host`, `floor-query-cost`), each a `run.sh` submitted from its own directory, with relative paths to the repo, the 1-in-100 subset, the Singularity cache and a shared `fmh-benchmark-data` storeDir (layout in its README), and the EBI Slurm settings in `site.config`. Work and outdir sit together in each run directory, as the hard-linked index publishing needs. Checked by a dry run of every script in a simulated layout and `nextflow config` on the merged configs.
  - Results: step 20 (all runs but `floor` error-free).

* **Phase 7, step 20 — step 19's ablation batch: results (HPC, main of 2026-10-05, runs ending 10:29–11:43; `run.json` records no commit; `results/ablations-v1`, the export minus `units/` and `mgnify/`, which stay in the 17 GB tarball).** Every run finished except `floor` error-free (`--iss_mode perfect`), still running. Numbers are means over 10 seeds; detection at `min_hits` 1 unless stated. Abundance on KO/Pfam units is `abundance_zi` (as in phase 5, step 3; `coverage_em` is per-copy depth, so on KO units its L1 is ~1.5 by construction); on MGnify90 units summed per Pfam it is the default `coverage_em` (all estimators agree there within 0.03).
  - **Index size at 1 in 100** (`du`, GB): sparse 0.77, `tbase01` 1.47, `tbase02` 1.86, `dense02` 1.99, `tbase02_all` 2.22, `tbase05` 2.58.
  - **Query cost** (`floor-query-cost`, ERR7738575 + ERR7746321 pooled, wall s / peak anonymous GB):

    | Pairs | sparse | `tbase01` | `tbase02` | `tbase02_all` | `tbase05` | `dense02` |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | 4 M | 9 / 0.7 | 12 / 1.0 | 16 / 1.2 | 19 / 1.4 | 25 / 1.4 | 24 / 2.7 |
    | 40 M | 86 / 0.9 | 87 / 1.4 | 101 / 1.5 | 98 / 1.7 | 122 / 2.2 | 194 / 21.9 |
    | 100 M | 200 / 1.1 | 208 / 1.5 | 212 / 1.7 | 230 / 2.1 | 254 / 2.5 | 479 / 54.0 |

    The dense pass alone is 265 s at 100 M pairs, and its memory grows with pairs (it holds read ids). Floors grow pass 1's hit table (39 M rows sparse, 285 M `tbase02`, 680 M `tbase05`) but stay within 2.5 GB, and EM, presence and gather converge as before. At 26.4 M reads, `dense02~edges20` profiles were OOM-killed 10 times before a retry passed; no floor index was.
  - **Rule 1, `aai` (`floor` NovaSeq, plain).** Raw bias at 90 / 80 / 70%: `dense02` +0.047 / +0.082 / +0.143; `tbase02` +0.050 / +0.103 / +0.196; `tbase02_all` +0.040 / +0.075 / +0.138; `tbase05` +0.030 / +0.071 / +0.140. Spearman, each on its own units: 0.533, 0.124, 0.434, 0.423 (`aai_n` 5,032, 5,641, 8,407, 9,829). The floors give an `aai` to more, and more divergent, units, so the Spearman values are not comparable. On the 2,853 units per sample that all four report (offline, from the profiles): Spearman 0.463 / 0.328 / 0.450 / 0.425, bias at 70% +0.165 / +0.182 / +0.161 / +0.164. *Calibrated* (held-out half, all four runs): `tbase02_all` and `tbase05` stay within ±0.01 at 70–95% in every run, with coverage 0.92–1.0. `tbase02` does not calibrate (−0.10 at 80% NovaSeq; −0.12 at 90% and +0.17 at 70% at 1.65 M reads): its eight-best-by-score k-mers bias `aai` in a way that varies by unit. `dense02` misses at 70% in the 26.4 M run (−0.054). **Rule 1: `tbase02_all` passes; `tbase05` misses Spearman by 0.008 (−0.038 against −0.03); `tbase02` fails.**
  - **Rule 2, detection at the MGnify90 level.** At `kmers_unique` ≥ 1 (NovaSeq), completeness\_90 is sparse 0.337, `tbase02` 0.644, `tbase02_all` 0.654, `tbase05` 0.741 (recall at 80–90% identity 0.276 → 0.488 / 0.574 / 0.617), but purity\_nearest falls (0.154 → 0.136 / 0.097 / 0.127). The floors detect 2–4× as many units per sample (46.8 k → 96.5 k / 195 k / 147 k), most of them near hits. At a threshold of 2 distinct k-mers (offline, NovaSeq), every floor beats sparse-at-1 on all three metrics: completeness\_90 0.543 / 0.578 / 0.678, purity\_nearest 0.228 / 0.154 / 0.217, purity\_near 0.435 / 0.373 / 0.450, against 0.337 / 0.154 / 0.323. The other three runs show the same pattern at 1 (completeness\_90 +0.26 to +0.42, purity\_nearest −0.02 to −0.06). **Rule 2 as written (at 1): every floor fails purity\_nearest. At matched thresholds every floor passes.**
  - **Rule 3, Pfam.** *`index` run (KO/Pfam units against base):* Pfam purity / completeness / Spearman / L1: base 0.974 / 0.669 / 0.843 / 0.627; `pfam_tbase02` 0.984 / 0.903 / 0.950 / 0.324; `pfam_tbase02_all` 0.977 / 0.911 / 0.951 / 0.324; `pfam_tbase05` 0.981 / 0.933 / 0.960 / 0.302; `pfam_dense02` 0.974 / 0.669 / 0.968 / 0.402. Every floor passes, on every metric. *`floor` runs (MGnify90 units summed per Pfam, NovaSeq):* sparse at 1: 0.741 / 0.739 / 0.796 / 0.546 (`dense02` the same detection, L1 0.570). At 1 the floors lose purity (`tbase02` 0.684, `tbase02_all` 0.632, `tbase05` 0.640) for completeness (0.813, 0.855, 0.852). At 2, `tbase02` is 0.762 / 0.751 / 0.838 / 0.500, better than sparse-at-1 on all four; `tbase02_all` (0.707 / 0.810) and `tbase05` (0.715 / 0.806) are still below it on purity. The other runs agree. `min_hits` 3 is added to `floor.config` so a `-resume` rescore (SCORE only) shows whether they reach it.
  - **Rule 4, host (`host`, KO).** False positives per sample at 0 / 50 / 90 / 99% host: base raw 111 / 104 / 82 / 130, mask 110 / 89 / 47 / 17, hostile 111 / 89 / 46 / 14; `tbase02` raw 191 / 172 / 134 / 204, mask 189 / 157 / 96 / 61, hostile 191 / 157 / 95 / 55. `tbase02` has more false positives at every share, including 0% host (it detects 1,870 more KOs per sample). Host-caused false positives at 99% (raw minus mask) are 113 for base and 143 for `tbase02` on KO; on Pfam they are 69 and 60, and `pfam_tbase02` has fewer false positives than `pfam_base` at every share. Purity under host is higher for the floor (KO at 99%: 0.947 against 0.914), and completeness at 99% host is 0.79 against 0.30. **Rule 4 as written (absolute counts against base) fails on KO and passes on Pfam. The floor does not make the index more host-prone: the extra false positives are there without host too.** `tbase02_all` and `tbase05` were not in this run.
    - *Mask against hostile:* within 6 false positives and 0.006 completeness at every share, with no loss at 0% host. `host_like_detected` is 0 in every mask arm. **The mask does what upstream removal does on these data: ship it.**
    - *Decoy arms are invalid.* The decoy role (Additional references, phase 10) is not built, so every detected human protein in the joint query was scored as a predicted label: +14 / +600 / +1,200 / +1,330 false positives per sample on base, and up to +10,600 on `tbase02`. `read_profile` in `bench.py` now drops units with `source` > 0 (the benchmark's only extra index is the decoy; test in `test_sample_truth_and_score`). A `-resume` of `host` re-runs SCORE alone. The export has no `profiles/`, so this cannot be rescored here.
  - **Rule 5, cost.** Query time and memory at 100 M pairs: every floor passes (≤ 254 s and 2.5 GB, against 479 s and 54 GB). Index size: `tbase02` passes (1.86 against 1.99 GB); `tbase02_all` (+12%) and `tbase05` (+30%) fail.
  - **Decision under step 19's rule: no floor passes, so the dense tier stays.** `tbase02` fails rule 1; `tbase02_all` and `tbase05` fail rule 5 on index size, and rules 2–3 at `min_hits` 1. Each failure in rules 2–3 is the threshold shift above: at 1, the floors call more units at lower purity. Compared at matched thresholds, they dominate. **Proposed amendment (not yet adopted; for the user to decide):** compare detection at matched purity (thresholds 1–3), and weigh index size against query memory. Then the dense tier goes. The floor is `tbase02_all` if containment `aai` ships: the only arm passing rule 1, and it calibrates in every run. It costs 12% more disk than `dense02` and 26× less query memory. If `aai` does not ship, the floor is `tbase02`: the smallest, and it dominates sparse at 2 on every detection and Pfam metric. Before adopting it: the `min_hits` 3 rescore for rule 3 at the MGnify90 level, `tbase02_all` in the host run, and its full-release size from the statistics pass (`--cost_args ... --t-base 0.02`).
  - **Read QC (`reads`): no built-in mask by default.** fastp-then-raw equals raw to three decimals in both read models: InSilicoSeq simulates no adapters, and poly-G is not in these reads. So step 1's rule (the mask at the *q* that matches fastp) gives *q* = 0. On NovaSeq, *q* 10 changes nothing, and *q* 20 / 30 cost 0.04 / 0.22 KO completeness on base. On MiSeq, *q* 10–30 trade completeness for purity (*q* 20: +0.005 purity, −0.029 completeness). `--min-qual` stays at 0 and external QC stays optional. Adapter read-through is untested here, since the simulator has none.
  - **Frame mode (`reads`): edges helps, decision pending.** `edges20` against stop-free on base: NovaSeq +0.026 KO completeness, −0.005 purity; MiSeq +0.064 completeness, −0.013 purity, L1 0.596 → 0.516. On `tbase02` MiSeq: completeness 0.969 → 0.992, L1 0.306 → 0.152, Spearman 0.934 → 0.980. MiSeq's errors make stops that drop reads whole, which edges recovers. At `min_hits` 2 the purity loss is ≤ 0.004 for +0.03–0.07 completeness. `edges30` is as good as `edges20`, with slightly better purity and L1; `all` matches `edges15` on detection but has worse L1 on `tbase02` (0.213 MiSeq). Query time is +15–25%. Step 1's rule asks for the short-protein abundance error, which the benchmark does not report, so the default stays stop-free for now. `edges30` is the candidate.
  - **Index factors (`index`, KO, against base 0.979 / 0.729).** *k* 9 / 10 gain completeness (0.757 / 0.735) for purity (0.959 / 0.974); *k* 12 loses completeness (0.724). Murphy-10 (*k* 13 / 15) and Dayhoff (*k* 17 / 20) give nothing *k* 9–11 does not. *n\_min* 16 is 0.969 / 0.783, and any floor dominates it. *t\_cap* 0.05 / 0.1 lose ≤ 0.007 completeness and gain ≤ 0.005 purity, a candidate cost cut (fewer sampled k-mers per read); at the MGnify90 level, where *t\_g* reaches the cap (median 0.074), it is untested. **Keep *k* 11, the protein alphabet and *n\_min* 8.** The dense tier on KO units (`dense02`/`05`/`10`) improves abundance only (L1 0.551 → 0.249 / 0.225 / 0.218), and `tbase02` does better at 0.180.
  - **Anomaly: one corrupt `dense02` profile** (`floor-26.4M`, seed 1, plain). It has the right 89,037 hit units, but every unit has the same hits (7), reads (3) and `kmers_dense` (295,213): per-unit aggregates were broadcast as one value. This inflated that run's `dense02` completeness\_90 (0.375 against sparse's 0.366; every other seed matches sparse exactly) and made its `aai_spearman` NaN. The task completed normally (210 s, 8.8 GB); its edges20 twin was among the OOM-killed tasks. Not reproduced. If the dense tier stays, this needs a look; if it goes, so does the code path.

* **Phase 7, step 21 — three release tiers, sized from step 20, and their benchmark run (set up, not run).** The dense tier is to go if step 20's amendment is adopted. The user asked for three index sizes: *slim*, about HUMAnN 4's database (~60 GB), for abundances without `aai`; *standard* (medium), for calibrated `aai`; *large*, up to 500 GB.
  - *Full-scale sizes* are extrapolated ×100 from the 1-in-100 builds (`meta.json` stats; the subset is phase 6's nested all-biome 1 in 100, 15.4 M of ~1.54×10⁹ clusters). Integer widths are set for full scale: unit values become u64 (35 bits), and bucket offsets become u64 (more than 2³² keys). "Compact" adds three format changes, none of them built yet: one copy of the needed unit columns (~30 B per unit, against ~99 B now for parquet plus `.npy`); *p\_in* in its own u8 array, so values stay u32; and two-level bucket offsets (~2 B per bucket). Shared k-mers grow ~4× per decade, which adds sets and also adds promiscuous drops, so these figures carry ±20–30% until the whole-release statistics (`cost-full`) report.

    | Index | Today's format | Compact | Benchmarked at 1 in 100 |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | sparse | 87 GB | 46 GB | yes |
    | `tbase01` | 168 GB | 79 GB | yes |
    | `slim` (*t\_base* 0.01, singletons 0.001) | ~91 GB | ~50 GB | no (estimate) |
    | `tbase02` | 204 GB | 100 GB | yes; `aai` does not calibrate |
    | `tbase02_n16` (*n\_min* 16, oversample 2) | ~240 GB | ~120 GB | no (estimate) |
    | `tbase02_all` | 259 GB | 141 GB | yes |
    | `tbase05` | 287 GB | 153 GB | yes |
    | `tbase05_all` | ~310 GB | ~175 GB | no (estimate) |
    | `tbase10_all` | ~430 GB | ~260 GB | no (estimate) |

  - *Choice, pending the run:*
    - **Slim**: `slim`. Plain `tbase01` is ~80 GB even compact. Singletons are 73% of clusters, and the raised floor is what gives them postings (units with a posting go from 5.2 M to 10.2 M at 1 in 100). Leaving singletons at the sparse rate keeps the floor's gain for non-singletons at about sparse's size.
    - **Standard** (medium): `tbase02_all` (~140 GB), or `tbase02_n16` (~120 GB) if it keeps the `aai` calibration. `tbase02` is ~100 GB, but the 8 best k-mers by score bias `aai` per unit (step 20).
    - **Large**: `tbase05_all` (~175 GB compact, ~310 GB today). `tbase10_all` would fill ~430 GB today, but step 20's gains flatten from 0.02 to 0.05, and query cost grows with hit rows (`tbase05` had 17× sparse's hit rows at 1 in 100). It is in the run to test whether filling the space buys anything.
  - *Code:* `--t-base-singleton` (`IndexParams.t_base_singleton`, default None = *t\_base*) sets singletons' *t\_g*. Singletons are never floored, so they keep every candidate up to their rate. `index.base_rate` (the lower of the two) is the rate below which the base stratum is complete, and the query's census now uses it. The query reads build parameters older indexes lack as their defaults (`_index_params`). The option is in `AAI_CALIBRATION_PARAMS`, and `cost.predict_cost` and the partitioned build's options follow it. Test: `test_singletons_at_their_own_base_rate` (singletons at 0.05 and at 0.6 against *t\_base* 0.3 keep exactly their k-mers up to that rate; non-singletons unchanged).
  - *Run:* `ablations/tiers.config` (`run_ablations.sh tiers OUTDIR --aai_subset DIR`; `hpc/kfp-ablations/tiers-novaseq`, then `tiers-query-cost`): as `floor` NovaSeq, plain frames, `min_hits` 1–3, seven indexes (sparse, `tbase01`, `slim`, `tbase02_all`, `tbase02_n16`, `tbase05_all`, `tbase10_all`), each published for the query cost run. Checked: a stub run on the test profile (with a laptop resource cap) gives 14 profiles over 2 seeds and a calibration map per index; real builds of the fixture with each new option.
  - **Follow-up benchmarks, beyond this run**, roughly in order of value:
    1. *Rescores, no new queries:* `host` (decoy units no longer scored, step 20) and the four `floor` runs (`min_hits` 3), by re-submitting with `-resume`.
    2. *`floor` error-free* (running).
    3. *Calibration transfer* (step 17's criterion, step 19's rule): the NovaSeq map of the chosen standard/large index scored with `aai-calibrate --map` on the MiSeq, 1.65 M and 26.4 M runs. The profiles are in the step-20 tarball, so this can run offline.
    4. *Host on the chosen tiers*: rule 4 for `tbase02_all`/`_n16` and `tbase05_all`, with the rescored decoy arms. Needs MGnify-unit indexes in `host.config` (it has KO/Pfam units only).
    5. *Frame mode*: step 1's rule needs abundance error binned by protein length, which `bench.py` does not report yet. Add it, then score `edges30` against stop-free on the chosen tiers.
    6. *Read QC with adapters*: InSilicoSeq simulates none, so fastp against the mask is untested where it matters. Simulate short inserts with adapter read-through.
    7. *`t_cap` 0.1 with a floor at the MGnify90 level*: up to ~2× fewer lookups per read; untested where *t\_g* reaches the cap.
    8. *Whole-release statistics* (`cost-full` with *t\_base* 0.01/0.02/0.05 and the singleton rate) to replace these extrapolations, then a full build of one tier and a full-scale query cost.

* **Phase 7, step 22 — calibration transfer, offline (step 21's follow-up 3).** For `tbase02_all` and `tbase05`, the map `AAI_CALIBRATE` fitted on `floor` NovaSeq was scored with `aai-calibrate --map` on each other run's held-out clusters. The profiles came from the step-20 tarball, one run at a time. The local Polars hash split reproduces the HPC scores exactly when the NovaSeq map is scored on NovaSeq, so the held-out clusters are the ones HPC used. Calibrated bias (coverage) at 90 / 80 / 70% for the NovaSeq map, against each run's own map in brackets:

  | Run | `tbase02_all` | `tbase05` |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | MiSeq | −0.003 / −0.005 / −0.026 (0.96 / 0.94 / 0.94) [≤ 0.004] | −0.005 / −0.011 / −0.016 (0.98 / 0.94 / 0.92) [≤ 0.001] |
  | 1.65 M reads | +0.039 / +0.043 / +0.072 (0.99 / 0.99 / 0.98) [≤ 0.022] | +0.030 / +0.041 / +0.072 (0.99 / 0.98 / 0.97) [≤ 0.002] |
  | 26.4 M reads | −0.012 / −0.015 / −0.067 (0.94 / 0.92 / 0.92) [≤ 0.002] | −0.008 / −0.021 / −0.044 (0.96 / 0.92 / 0.90) [≤ 0.002] |

  - **Step 17's criterion (bias within ±0.01 at 70–100%, coverage ≥ 0.9) fails on depth for both indexes.** On read model, MiSeq passes at 80–95% and misses at 70% (−0.016 / −0.026). Coverage stays ≥ 0.9 everywhere, because the intervals are wide enough; the point estimates move.
  - *Why:* raw `aai` bias depends on depth. At 70% it is +0.16 at 1.65 M reads, +0.14 at NovaSeq's depth and +0.13 at 26.4 M (step 20, raw rows). A map from `aai` alone cannot fit all three. At low depth the zero-inflated copies estimate is high (`aai` = copies^(1/k)).
  - **Next** (superseded by step 23: the bias has mechanistic causes, so the fix is a model, not a fitted map): make the map depth-aware: fit it on pooled runs with the unit's depth (`coverage_zi`) as a second input, e.g. one inverse map per depth bin, interpolated. Score it with the same transfer test. Until then, a map shipped with an index holds only near the depth it was fitted at. This applies to every standard/large candidate, not to the choice between them.
  - **Check: per-unit coverage explains the shift.** Raw `aai` − true identity, binned by the unit's own `coverage_zi` and by true identity, for units nearest to one gene (all 10 seeds, both indexes, ~35–65 k units per run). At matched coverage the four runs agree within ~0.005 at 70–90% identity, and within ~0.01 at 90–95%; the exception is 1.65 M reads at coverage 0.5–2, which is up to 0.016 lower. Bias depends on the unit's coverage below ~2× and is flat above it. `tbase02_all` at 70–80%: +0.22 at coverage ≤ 0.5, +0.13 at 0.5–1, +0.12 at 1–2, +0.11 from 2 up; at 80–90%: +0.14, +0.08, +0.06, +0.05; at 90–95%: +0.075, +0.05, +0.03, +0.02. `tbase05` matches to ~0.01. The runs differ only in their mix of coverages: median `coverage_zi` is 0.8 at 1.65 M reads, 2.3 NovaSeq, 3.4 MiSeq and 8.5 at 26.4 M. So a map with the unit's `coverage_zi` as a second input should transfer between runs, and between samples of any total depth; the sample's read count is not a useful input.

* **Phase 7, step 23 — the causes of `aai` bias, and a model-based estimator to replace fitted calibration (analysis done; design agreed with the user; built in step 24).** Step 22's per-coverage map fixes the symptom by fitting to the benchmark. The user asked for an adjustment grounded in how k-mers, the index and the query behave instead. The analysis was offline on `floor` NovaSeq, `tbase02_all`, 10 seeds. Units with an `aai`, joined to MGnify90-level truth, ~52 k units; the profiles came from the step-20 tarball. Scripts were scratch only; redo them from this description.
  - **Mechanism 1 — real proteins keep many more exact k-mers than a^k (the main effect).** `aai` = copies^(1/k) assumes each k-mer survives independently with probability a^k. Units hit only by their nearest gene, coverage > 4, median `copies_zi` against true identity: 0.71 → 0.164 (a^11 = 0.024); 0.79 → 0.197 (0.072); 0.86 → 0.287 (0.195); 0.91 → 0.407 (0.359); 0.96 → 0.667 (0.653). Substitutions cluster in variable regions, so conserved stretches survive whole. A one-parameter *regional rate* model fits: each k-mer window evolves at one gamma-distributed rate of shape α, so survival S(a) = (1 + k (a^(−1/α) − 1))^(−α), with a = (1 + t/α)^(−α). RMSE over 0.71–0.99 is 0.027 at α = 1.5, 0.030 at 2 and 0.055 at 1; a^k is off by up to 0.14.
